    <div data-server-rendered="true" id="__nuxt"><!----><div id="__layout"><div><section data-v-6a702c08><div data-v-925a200c data-v-6a702c08><a class="back-ti-nav" data-v-925a200c>è¿å TI ä¸»é¡µ</a> <div class="nav-bar" data-v-925a200c><div class="ico-favicon" data-v-925a200c><div class="banner-above" data-v-925a200c><i class="banner-logo" data-v-925a200c></i> <div class="line" data-v-925a200c></div> <p class="text-first-line" data-v-925a200c>RESEARCH</p> <p class="banner-below" data-v-925a200c>æ° æ® é©± å¨ å® å¨</p></div></div></div></div> <div class="article-page" data-v-055d1340 data-v-6a702c08><div class="page-header" data-v-055d1340><div class="title-detail" data-v-4e3604fb data-v-055d1340>
    APT-C-36: Continuous Attacks Targeting Colombian Government Institutions and Corporations
  </div> <div class="author lh32" data-v-055d1340><p class="text-grey-12" data-v-055d1340> By 360å¨èææ¥ä¸­å¿ | äºä»¶è¿½è¸ª</p> <a title="ä¸è½½PDF" href="null" download="apt-c-36-continuous-attacks-targeting-colombian-government-institutions-and-corporations-en" class="dl-pdf" style="display:none" data-v-055d1340><i class="icon-dl-pdf icon-24" data-v-055d1340></i> <span class="dl-text" data-v-055d1340> PDF</span></a> <a title="ä¸è½½IOC" href="null" download="apt-c-36-continuous-attacks-targeting-colombian-government-institutions-and-corporations-en" class="dl-ioc" style="display:none" data-v-055d1340><i class="icon-dl-ioc icon-24" data-v-055d1340></i> <span class="dl-text" data-v-055d1340> IOC</span></a></div></div> <div class="main-body" data-v-055d1340><h2 class="line" data-line="0">Background</h2>
<p class="line" data-line="2">Since April 2018, an APT group (Blind Eagle, APT-C-36) suspected coming from <font color="#DC143C"> South America</font> carried out continuous targeted attacks against  <font color="#DC143C">Colombian </font>government institutions as well as important corporations in financial sector, petroleum industry, professional manufacturing, etc.</p>
<p class="line" data-line="4">Till this moment, 360 Threat Intelligence Center captured 29 bait documents, 62 Trojan samples and multiple related malicious domains in total. Attackers are targeting Windows platform and aiming at government institutions as well as big companies in Colombia.</p>
<p class="line" data-line="6">The first sample being captured was in April 2018 and since that we observed a lot more related ones. Attackers like to use spear-fishing email with password protected RAR attachment to avoid being detected by the email gateway. Decryption password is provided in the mail body and inside the attachment it is a MHTML macro based document with the .doc suffix. Its purpose is to implant Imminent backdoor and gain a foothold into the target network which may make the follow up lateral movement easier to implement.</p>
<p class="line" data-line="8">After analyzing the last modified time of the encrypted documents, character set (locale) of the MHTML files, author names used by attackers, as well as elements like geopolitics in APT attacks, 360 Threat Intelligence Center suspect <font color="#DC143C">attackers come from South America and are in the UTC -4 time zone (or adjacent ones).</font></p>
<h2 class="line" data-line="10">Target and Victim Analysis</h2>
<p class="line" data-line="12">After performing investigations on the classified victims, we find the attacker targets big companies and government agencies in Colombia. The purpose is to implant Imminent backdoor to gain a foothold into the target network which may make the follow up lateral movement easier to implement. Based upon victimsâ backgrounds, the attacker is focusing on strategic-level intelligence and may also have motivations to steal business intelligence and intellectual properties.</p>
<h3 class="line" data-line="14">Spoofed Source and Industry Distribution</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="16">Based on the statistics of the attack information collected by 360 Threat Intelligence Center, the attacker disguised as Colombian national institutions to attack government agencies, financial institutions, large domestic companies and multinational corporation branches in Colombia.</p>

<h3 class="line" data-line="43">Affected Targets</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="45">After monitoring and correlating the APT attack, 360 Threat Intelligence Center discovered multiple related emails to attack Colombian government agencies, financial institutions and large enterprises. Based upon the above work, we collected the following spear-fishing emails, bait documents and the corresponding victims.</p>
<h4 class="line" data-line="47">Ecopetrol</h4>
<ul>
<li><strong>Information and Related Email of the Attacked Corporation</strong></li>
</ul>
<p class="line" data-line="51">Ecopetrol, also known as Colombian Petroleum Co. (<a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20190625182633/http://www.ecopetrol.com.co/">www.ecopetrol.com.co</a>), is the largest and primary petroleum company in Colombia.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="53"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/31103e6081f5cca98126e844df4d54b8.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="text-center line" data-line="55">Targeted Email Attack Against Ecopetrol</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Related Bait Document</strong></li>
</ul>
<p class="line" data-line="59">The document was disguised as originating from the National Directorate of Taxes and Customs (www.dian.gov.co):</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="61"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/72a428e42a544aff95f1d5b3a618d2e7.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="text-center line" data-line="63">Dian Embargo Bancario # 609776.doc</p>
<h4 class="line" data-line="65">Hocol Petroleum Limited</h4>
<ul>
<li><strong>Information and Related Email of the Attacked Corporation</strong></li>
</ul>
<p class="line" data-line="69">Hocol was founded in 1956. It is a subsidiary of Ecopetrol and offers hydrocarbon exploration and production services.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="71"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/4bce54f6be443fff3da5c0d4139dff34.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="79"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/e96524decbafbc8a0dd66e4fd8116d80.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<h4 class="line" data-line="83">Logistics Company (Almaviva)</h4>
<ul>
<li><strong>Information and Related Email of the Attacked Corporation</strong></li>
</ul>
<p class="line" data-line="87">Almaviva is a Colombian logistics company, it optimizes the supply chain through the safe management of processes and tools to ensure the efficiency of logistics operations.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="89"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/d4ebf277620262ee050ae107561b70c5.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<ul>

</ul>
<p class="line" data-line="95">The attacker masquerades as the National Administrative Department of Statistics to launch the attack.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="97"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/20a6f8f944162cfa29d02aa026324a1e.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<p class="line" data-line="105">The Banco Agrario is a Colombian state financial institution founded in 1999 to provide banking services in the rural sectors.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="107"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/fc9796bb132761ff796d7e7d3f98cf54.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<h4 class="line" data-line="137">Bank in Colombia (Banco de Occidente)</h4>
<ul>
<li><strong>Information and Related Email of the Attacked Bank</strong></li>

<h2 class="line" data-line="209">Technical Details</h2>
<p class="line" data-line="211">360 Threat Intelligence Center conducted a detailed analysis of the attack process based on the common attack techniques used by the APT group.</p>
<h3 class="line" data-line="213">The Latest Attack</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="215">On February 14, 2019, 360 Threat Intelligence Center monitored attacks by the APT group again. The corresponding mail was not found by using the recently captured bait document (MD5:0c97d7f6a1835a3fe64c1c625ea109ed). However, after investigation we found another similar bait document (MD5: 3de286896c8eb68a21a6dcf7dae8ec97) and related target attack mail (MD5: f2d5cb747110b43558140c700dbf0e5e). The mail was disguised from the Colombian National Civil Registry and attacked the Colombian National Institute for the Blind.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="217"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/774665fae3b59c17fa681bed2916717e.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="text-center line" data-line="219">Recently captured bait document, disguised from the Colombian National Civil Registry (MD5: 0c97d7f6a1835a3fe64c1c625ea109ed)</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="221"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/e58a0a6aaaa4a72865e68adea501eca7.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="text-center line" data-line="223">Email attacking the Colombian National Institute for the Blind</p>
<h3 class="line" data-line="225">Spoofed Source and Detection Bypass</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="227">When attacking different targets, attackers carefully consider how to spoof the source of the message to make it look more credible. For example, by masquerading the National Civil Registry to attack the Institute for the Blind, pretending to be the Tax and Customs Administration to attack companies with international trade, disguising as the judiciary and immigration authorities against banks and multinational corporation branches located in Colombia.</p>
<p class="line" data-line="229">The attacker also carefully constructs the content of the message to appear originating from the forged institution and relating to the target. The following picture shows the translation of the corresponding mail disguised as originating from the judiciary of Colombia to attack the ATH Colombia branch.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="231"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/7625b52df007a67eb6e6c61a33a63464.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="233">The email attachment is encrypted and stored in the compressed package, and a decryption password is provided in the mail body to bypass the security detection of the email gateway.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="235"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/d70750399875aea91d69f791a535af5f.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="237">Decryption password provided in the email</p>
<p class="line" data-line="239">After analyzing the mail, we found that the attacker used approaches such as proxy and VPN to hide its IP address when sending emails. So the senderâs real IP has not yet been obtained, only to figure out that these messages are sent through IDCs in Florida, USA. Some related IP addresses are as follows:</p>
<p class="line" data-line="241">128.90.106.22</p>
<p class="line" data-line="243">128.90.107.21</p>
<p class="line" data-line="245">128.90.107.189</p>
<p class="line" data-line="247">128.90.107.236</p>
<p class="line" data-line="249">128.90.108.126</p>
<p class="line" data-line="251">128.90.114.5</p>
<p class="line" data-line="253">128.90.115.28</p>
<p class="line" data-line="255">128.90.115.179</p>

<p class="line" data-line="275">When the macro code gets executed, it calls the Document_Open function automatically.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="277"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/e9dda845961480ac73da18671a61c279.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="279">Function Document_Open first calls the Main function to download binary data from hxxp://diangovcomuiscia.com/media/a.jpg and save as %AppData%\1.exe (MD5: ef9f19525e7862fb71175c0bbfe74247).</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="281"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/28a7de6f974a06c0217ca135df372e56.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<li><strong>Dynamic Debugging</strong></li>

<p class="line" data-line="386">The core component will check whether it is located in the %temp%\[appname] directory, otherwise it copies itself to %temp%\[appname]\[appname] and set the file attribute to hidden.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="388"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/1d250fc86c4c7e7771de1755466f7c73.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="392"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/73aa011b5cb27a5c34d812d573391412.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="394">Finally delete the original file and exit the process:</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="396"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/01b7e8c43ec2bdc6cfe9c9e980ec8fa3.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="398">When the copied file gets executed, it creates the Imminent directory in the %AppData% directory to save the encrypted log, network information and system information. The file will be uploaded to C2 when related command is received.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="400"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/9606e822b126644a041cdb06d807fbf7.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="402">C2: mentes.publicvm.com:4050</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="404"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/263a35e0c0fc11b23b17c1dfe9201c26.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>

<h2 class="line" data-line="423">Attribution</h2>
<p class="line" data-line="425">After analyzing the last modified time of the encrypted documents, character set (locale) of the MHTML files, as well as elements like geopolitics in APT attacks, 360 Threat Intelligence Center suspect attackers are in the UTC -4 time zone (or adjacent ones).</p>
<h3 class="line" data-line="427">The Reliable Last Modified Time</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="429">Since RAR will save the modified time of the file, the time of the document obtained after decryption is very reliable. Take password protected RAR archive (Registraduria Nacional del Estado Civil -Proceso inicado.rar) as an example, the time after decryption is the same as the left one located in the MHTML meta data (the last modified time on the right side needs to be reduced by 8 hours since we are in the UTC +8 time zone).</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="431"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/a96a70298d69ac2a3723cdf346560f81.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="line" data-line="433">By comparing each last modified time of the RAR archive with the one located in the meta data, we have confidence to say that the time is not spoofed. So it makes sense to perform related statistics of all the bait documents captured.</p>
<h3 class="line" data-line="435">Statistics of the Last Modified Time</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="437">All of the last modified time from the captured bait documents are shown in the table below:</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><strong>UTC+00</strong></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>00:32</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>01:15</td>

<p class="line" data-line="472">From the above we could see that the time never distributed between 05:30 and 12:30, which supposed to be sleep hours. Combining with the fact that most of the activities are between 13:00 and 2:00, we suspect attackers are in the UTC -4 time zone (or adjacent ones).</p>

<h3 class="line" data-line="511">Language and Charset</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="513">We also perform statistics on the language and charset of the bait documents (MHTML) and find they are created on Western European language environment (Spanish, etc.).</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="515"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/6faff1d0db79aa65e51c9fd2fb71b599.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="text-center line" data-line="517">Charsetï¼windows-1252</p>
<p class="line" data-line="519">Some of the author information are also Spanish.</p>
<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="521"><img src="/web/20190625182633im_/https://ti.360.net/uploads/2019/02/18/b23d74d9fa0f2e14b3d5c3f1e5b2271b.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>
<p class="text-center line" data-line="523">Centro de Servicios Judiciales</p>
<h3 class="line" data-line="525">Attacker Profile</h3>
<p class="line" data-line="527">Based on the time zone of the attacker, the language being used, and the geopolitical factors of the APT attack, we come up with following findings:</p>
<ol>
<li>
<p>The time zone (UTC -4) is related to countries in South America.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Most of the countries in South America use Spanish (except Brazil), which matches the attackerâs locale and user names in the bait documents.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>APT attack could probably be carried out by neighboring countries.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>The background of the victims and the duration of the attack indicate the attacker keeps concerned with strategic-level intelligence for a long time.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<p class="line" data-line="537">Above all, 360 Threat Intelligence Center suspect the APT group probably comes from South American countries with government support.</p>

Attackers are targeting Windows platform and aiming at government institutions as well as big companies in Colombia.",a.author="360å¨èææ¥ä¸­å¿",a.headImg="/uploads/2017/08/30/5deacf706bb3b7d9db9096b6d3964641.png",a.descImg=n,a.pdfFile=n,a.iocFile=n,a.tags=["APT-C-36","BLIND EAGLE","APT"],a.publish_time="2019-02-18T19:14:32.000Z",a.content='<h2 class="line" data-line="0">Background</h2>\n<p class="line" data-line="2">Since April 2018, an APT group (Blind Eagle, APT-C-36) suspected coming from <font color=#DC143C > South America</font> carried out continuous targeted attacks against  <font color=#DC143C >Colombian </font>government institutions as well as important corporations in financial sector, petroleum industry, professional manufacturing, etc.</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="4">Till this moment, 360 Threat Intelligence Center captured 29 bait documents, 62 Trojan samples and multiple related malicious domains in total. Attackers are targeting Windows platform and aiming at government institutions as well as big companies in Colombia.</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="6">The first sample being captured was in April 2018 and since that we observed a lot more related ones. Attackers like to use spear-fishing email with password protected RAR attachment to avoid being detected by the email gateway. Decryption password is provided in the mail body and inside the attachment it is a MHTML macro based document with the .doc suffix. Its purpose is to implant Imminent backdoor and gain a foothold into the target network which may make the follow up lateral movement easier to implement.</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="8">After analyzing the last modified time of the encrypted documents, character set (locale) of the MHTML files, author names used by attackers, as well as elements like geopolitics in APT attacks, 360 Threat Intelligence Center suspect <font color=#DC143C >attackers come from South America and are in the UTC -4 time zone (or adjacent ones).</font></p>\n<h2 class="line" data-line="10">Target and Victim Analysis</h2>\n<p class="line" data-line="12">After performing investigations on the classified victims, we find the attacker targets big companies and government agencies in Colombia. The purpose is to implant Imminent backdoor to gain a foothold into the target network which may make the follow up lateral movement easier to implement. Based upon victimsâ backgrounds, the attacker is focusing on strategic-level intelligence and may also have motivations to steal business intelligence and intellectual properties.</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="14">Spoofed Source and Industry Distribution</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="16">Based on the statistics of the attack information collected by 360 Threat Intelligence Center, the attacker disguised as Colombian national institutions to attack government agencies, financial institutions, large domestic companies and multinational corporation branches in Colombia.</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Spoofed Source</td>\n<td>Target</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Colombian National Civil Registry</td>\n<td>INCI (Colombian National Institute for the Blind)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>National Directorate of Taxes and Customs</td>\n<td>Ecopetrol (Colombian Petroleum Co.)<br>Hocol (Subsidiary of Ecopetrol)<br>Wheel manufacturer in Colombia (IMSA)<br>Byington Colombia</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>National Administrative Department of Statistics</td>\n<td>Logistics company in Colombia (Almaviva)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Colombian National Cyber Police</td>\n<td>Bank in Colombia (Banco de Occidente)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Office of the Attorney General</td>\n<td>ATH Columbia Division<br>Bank in Colombia (Banco de Occidente)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Colombia Migration</td>\n<td>Sun Chemical Columbia Branch</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<p class="line" data-line="28">Some malicious domains used by the attacker also masquerade as Colombian government websites. For example, âdiangovcomuiscia.comâ looks like the official one âmuiscia.dian.gov.coâ that belongs to the National Directorate of Taxes and Customs.</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="30">The attacker also forged the company information in the Imminent RAT:</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Company Information in RAT</td>\n<td>Company Description</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Abbott Laboratories</td>\n<td>A healthcare company based in the United States</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Chevron</td>\n<td>A multinational energy company in the United States</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Energizer Holdings Inc.</td>\n<td>American battery manufacturer</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Progressive Corporation</td>\n<td>Auto insurance provider in America</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Simon Property Group Inc</td>\n<td>A commercial real estate company in America</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Sports Authority Inc</td>\n<td>A sports goods retailer in the United States</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Strongeagle, Lda.</td>\n<td>A company related to law suit in Portugal</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="43">Affected Targets</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="45">After monitoring and correlating the APT attack, 360 Threat Intelligence Center discovered multiple related emails to attack Colombian government agencies, financial institutions and large enterprises. Based upon the above work, we collected the following spear-fishing emails, bait documents and the corresponding victims.</p>\n<h4 class="line" data-line="47">Ecopetrol</h4>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Information and Related Email of the Attacked Corporation</strong></li>\n</ul>\n<p class="line" data-line="51">Ecopetrol, also known as Colombian Petroleum Co. Its business credit information services include business and credit information, commercial collection, and marketing services.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="197"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/5a6d31cba819df653fb91a38b4647dab.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="text-center line" data-line="199">Targeted Email Attack Against Byington</p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Related Bait Document</strong></li>\n</ul>\n<p class="line" data-line="203">The document was disguised as originating from the National Directorate of Taxes and Customs:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="205"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/271235dfa6c1272bbd88fcced1fb3df7.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="text-center line" data-line="207">estado de cuenta.doc</p>\n<h2 class="line" data-line="209">Technical Details</h2>\n<p class="line" data-line="211">360 Threat Intelligence Center conducted a detailed analysis of the attack process based on the common attack techniques used by the APT group.</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="213">The Latest Attack</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="215">On February 14, 2019, 360 Threat Intelligence Center monitored attacks by the APT group again. The corresponding mail was not found by using the recently captured bait document (MD5:0c97d7f6a1835a3fe64c1c625ea109ed). However, after investigation we found another similar bait document (MD5: 3de286896c8eb68a21a6dcf7dae8ec97) and related target attack mail (MD5: f2d5cb747110b43558140c700dbf0e5e). The mail was disguised from the Colombian National Civil Registry and attacked the Colombian National Institute for the Blind.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="217"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/774665fae3b59c17fa681bed2916717e.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="text-center line" data-line="219">Recently captured bait document, disguised from the Colombian National Civil Registry (MD5: 0c97d7f6a1835a3fe64c1c625ea109ed)</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="221"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/e58a0a6aaaa4a72865e68adea501eca7.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="text-center line" data-line="223">Email attacking the Colombian National Institute for the Blind</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="225">Spoofed Source and Detection Bypass</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="227">When attacking different targets, attackers carefully consider how to spoof the source of the message to make it look more credible. For example, by masquerading the National Civil Registry to attack the Institute for the Blind, pretending to be the Tax and Customs Administration to attack companies with international trade, disguising as the judiciary and immigration authorities against banks and multinational corporation branches located in Colombia.</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="229">The attacker also carefully constructs the content of the message to appear originating from the forged institution and relating to the target. The following picture shows the translation of the corresponding mail disguised as originating from the judiciary of Colombia to attack the ATH Colombia branch.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="231"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/7625b52df007a67eb6e6c61a33a63464.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="233">The email attachment is encrypted and stored in the compressed package, and a decryption password is provided in the mail body to bypass the security detection of the email gateway.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="235"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/d70750399875aea91d69f791a535af5f.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="237">Decryption password provided in the email</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="239">After analyzing the mail, we found that the attacker used approaches such as proxy and VPN to hide its IP address when sending emails. So the senderâs real IP has not yet been obtained, only to figure out that these messages are sent through IDCs in Florida, USA. Some related IP addresses are as follows:</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="241">128.90.106.22</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="243">128.90.107.21</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="245">128.90.107.189</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="247">128.90.107.236</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="249">128.90.108.126</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="251">128.90.114.5</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="253">128.90.115.28</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="255">128.90.115.179</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="257">The Bait Document</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="259">All of the bait documents are MHTML ones with malicious macro embedded and the .doc suffix to bypass detection. Below is an example of bait document captured by 360 Threat Intelligence Center in February 2019:</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>File Name</td>\n<td>Registraduria Nacional - Notificacion cancelacion cedula de ciudadania.doc</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>MD5</td>\n<td>0c97d7f6a1835a3fe64c1c625ea109ed</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Forged Source</td>\n<td>The Colombian National Civil Registry</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="267"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/eb6686a669198e291312249e35089e05.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="269">MHTML macro based document with the .doc suffix</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="271"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/d61506fc4ed8f28b2034e3946f7c2474.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="273">The document is disguised from the Colombian National Civil Registry and uses Spanish to prompt the victim to enable the macro code in order to execute the subsequent payload.</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="275">When the macro code gets executed, it calls the Document_Open function automatically.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="277"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/e9dda845961480ac73da18671a61c279.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="279">Function Document_Open first calls the Main function to download binary data from hxxp://diangovcomuiscia.com/media/a.jpg and save as %AppData%\\1.exe (MD5: ef9f19525e7862fb71175c0bbfe74247).</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="281"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/28a7de6f974a06c0217ca135df372e56.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="283">Then calls the fcL4qOb4 function to set the scheduled task and disguise as the one used by Google:</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Author</td>\n<td>Google Inc</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Description (after translation)</td>\n<td>This task stops the Google Telemetry Agent, that examines and uploads information about the use and errors of Google solutions when a user logs in to the system.</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Task Action</td>\n<td>Launch %AppData%\\1.exe</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Task Definition</td>\n<td>GoogleUpdate</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<p class="line" data-line="292">The relevant code is shown below:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="294"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/1121a1ae6f4493ec39207a3b719dddb6.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="296">Payloadï¼Imminentï¼</h3>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>File Name</td>\n<td>1.exe</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>MD5</td>\n<td>ef9f19525e7862fb71175c0bbfe74247</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Compiler</td>\n<td>.NET</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<p class="line" data-line="304">The backdoor payload (1.exe) get dropped out is in C# with obfuscation:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="306"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/115c0bb2ad26f596919d46cce8b48f8b.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="308">After deobfuscation you can see âImminent Monitorâ string which may indicate it is related to Imminent Monitor RAT:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="310"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/8ad8769ab9356df123c57c61efe2c04a.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="312">When get executed, it first extracts resource named as &quot;application&quot; and decrypt to a legitimate lzma.dll library:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="314"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/dde2665fc91bd4f9f67a9c43fbb59db2.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="316"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/1bf95f8688f52f2a7adba56827e48f9a.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="318"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/81088024c234cbc0bfed7cd79ba65adb.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="320">Then extract resource named as &quot;_7z&quot;, and decompress it with lzma.dll to get the Imminent Monitor RAT (MD5: 4fd291e3319eb3433d91ee24cc39102e).</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="322"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/8587db21cd130940c627652dd41ac5fc.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="324"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/425d2797c5fe502e3c7f3e59d8910c98.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<h4 class="line" data-line="326">Core Component</h4>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>MD5</td>\n<td>4fd291e3319eb3433d91ee24cc39102e</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Static Analysis</strong></li>\n</ul>\n<p class="line" data-line="334">It is a variant of Imminent Monitor RAT while obfuscated by ConfuserEx and Eazfuscator.NET:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="336"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/ed70a043487803a0407f6a0da7c271a8.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="338">After partially removing the obfuscation, it can be seen that the backdoor supports below functions:</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>ID</td>\n<td>Function</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>bDfBqxDCINCfwSAfMnZwspLefnc</td>\n<td>Host src="/uploads/2019/02/18/a9d2c0e545915618b7f924d70d013f57.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="380">It is consistent with the descriptions provided on the official website:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="382"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/7a686ba2e95e681370f3cb515c22b003.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Dynamic Debugging</strong></li>\n</ul>\n<p class="line" data-line="386">The core component will check whether it is located in the %temp%\\[appname] directory, otherwise it copies itself to %temp%\\[appname]\\[appname] and set the file attribute to hidden.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="388"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/1d250fc86c4c7e7771de1755466f7c73.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="390">Then launch the copied file:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="392"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/73aa011b5cb27a5c34d812d573391412.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="394">Finally delete the original file and exit the process:</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="396"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/01b7e8c43ec2bdc6cfe9c9e980ec8fa3.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="398">When the copied file gets executed, it creates the Imminent directory in the %AppData% directory to save the encrypted log, network information and system information. The file will be uploaded to C2 when related command is received.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="400"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/9606e822b126644a041cdb06d807fbf7.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="402">C2: mentes.publicvm.com:4050</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="404"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/263a35e0c0fc11b23b17c1dfe9201c26.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<h2 class="line" data-line="406">TTPs (Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures)</h2>\n<p class="line" data-line="408">360 Threat Intelligence Center summarized TTPs of the APT group as follows:</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Attack corporations;<br>4.Commercial Trojan Imminent is used to remotely control the target;</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<h2 class="line" data-line="423">Attribution</h2>\n<p class="line" data-line="425">After analyzing the last modified time of the encrypted documents, character set (locale) of the MHTML files, as well as elements like geopolitics in APT attacks, 360 Threat Intelligence Center suspect attackers are in the UTC -4 time zone (or adjacent ones).</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="427">The Reliable Last Modified Time</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="429">Since RAR will save the modified time of the file, the time of the document obtained after decryption is very reliable. Take password protected RAR archive (Registraduria Nacional del Estado Civil -Proceso inicado.rar) as an example, the time after decryption is the same as the left one located in the MHTML meta data (the last modified time on the right side needs to be reduced by 8 hours since we are in the UTC +8 time zone).</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="431"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/a96a70298d69ac2a3723cdf346560f81.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="line" data-line="433">By comparing each last modified time of the RAR archive with the one located in the meta data, we have confidence to say that the time is not spoofed. So it makes sense to perform related statistics of all the bait documents captured.</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="435">Statistics of the Last Modified Time</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="437">All of the last modified time from the captured bait documents are shown in the table below:</p>\n<table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th></th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>UTC+00</strong></td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>00:32</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>01:15</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>01:15</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>01:17</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>01:35</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>01:59</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>02:57</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>03:28</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>04:40</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>04:55</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><font color=#DC143C >05:17</font></td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><font color=#DC143C >12:27</font></td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>12:49</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>12:50</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>13:38</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>13:42</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>13:49</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>14:21</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>14:22</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>15:19</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>15:26</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>15:30</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>15:56</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>17:22</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>17:58</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>18:31</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>20:53</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>21:31</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>23:30</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n<p class="line" data-line="472">From the above we could see that the time never distributed between 05:30 and 12:30, which supposed to be sleep hours. Combining with the fact that most of the activities are between 13:00 and 2:00, we suspect attackers are in the UTC -4 time zone (or adjacent ones).</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="474">PE data-line="511">Language and Charset</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="513">We also perform statistics on the language and charset of the bait documents (MHTML) and find they are created on Western European language environment (Spanish, etc.).</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="515"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/6faff1d0db79aa65e51c9fd2fb71b599.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="text-center line" data-line="517">Charsetï¼windows-1252</p>\n<p class="line" data-line="519">Some of the author information are also Spanish.</p>\n<p class="line img-box text-center" data-line="521"><img src="/uploads/2019/02/18/b23d74d9fa0f2e14b3d5c3f1e5b2271b.png" alt="" width="70%"></p>\n<p class="text-center line" data-line="523">Centro de Servicios Judiciales</p>\n<h3 class="line" data-line="525">Attacker Profile</h3>\n<p class="line" data-line="527">Based on the time zone of the attacker, the language being used, and the geopolitical factors of the APT attack, we come up with following findings:</p>\n<ol>\n<li>\n<p>The time zone (UTC -4) is related to countries in South America.</p>\n</li>\n<li>\n<p>Most of the countries in South America use Spanish (except Brazil), which matches the attackerâs locale and user names in the bait documents.</p>\n</li>\n<li>\n<p>APT attack could probably be carried out by neighboring countries.</p>\n</li>\n<li>\n<p>The background of the victims and the duration of the attack indicate the attacker keeps concerned with strategic-level intelligence for a long time.</p>\n</li>\n</ol>\n<p class="line" data-line="537">Above all, 360 Threat Intelligence Center suspect the APT group probably comes from South American countries with government support.</p>\n<h2 class="line"
